When Bharata heard the narration of his brother’s tale, he abandoned the idea of ending his life. Hope had returned to Ayodhya, and the messenger of hope had saved every member of Lord Rama’s family from disaster. He had saved Mother Sita by bringing Her the message of Lord Rama; he had saved Lord Rama by bringing him the news of Mother Sita’s safety; he had saved Lakshmana by bringing the magical herbs from the Himalayas, and now, he had saved Bharata by bringing the news of Lord Rama’s return.188

When Hanuman broke the news of Rama having reached the ashram of Bharadwaja muni that was very close to Nandigram where Bharata was currently located, He carefully observed the eyes of the king of Ayodhya. Instantly, there was a sparkle in His eyes. His eyes teared up with joy, and He just couldn’t hide his happiness which was so expressive and evident. Hanuman did not find a single trace of doubt, resentment or insecurity. He only found joy, gratitude, relief and excitement in them. Hanuman was convinced that Bharata was indeed a dharmatma, just as the dying Vali was. Announcing that Rama would soon be there in Ayodhya, Hanuman departed back to Bharadwaja muni’s ashram to share his findings with Rama.189

When he returned to the ashram, the whole vanara army was in the midst of eating a sumptuous feast arranged by Bharadwaja muni. When they saw Hanuman walk in, the organizers were trying to find a leaf plate for Hanuman to have his meal. But unfortunately they had run short of leaf plates. Seeing the awkward situation, Rama waved His hand and invited Hanuman to sit in front of his plate. With his index finger Rama drew a line on his leaf plate and created a partition in it. He made Hanuman sit right in front of him and asked him to eat from the other half of the leaf as He continued to eat from His side of the leaf. Everyone was amazed by Rama’s love for Hanuman.190

After the meal, Hanuman explained his observation of Bharata and conveyed to Rama that there could not be a more loving and selfless brother than Bharata. He had absolutely no iota of desire to rule the kingdom and was dying for Rama to return. Rama immediately set out to mount the pushpak vimana as He could no longer bear the separation from His loving brother.

brothers’ reunion

His joy knew no bounds. His face lit up. The news of the return of his brother exhilarated him. He expressed his joy by donating hundred thousand cows to hundred villages. As soon as he got the news, he had ordered Shatrughna to rush to Ayodhya and convey the good news to their guru Vasistha, the mothers and the most importantly the citizens of Ayodhya who had been waiting patiently like the chakora bird does for the first drops of rain. When word spread in Ayodhya, celebrations erupted everywhere. People spontaneously began to light their homes with lamps and decorate their courtyards to welcome their returning prince. They dressed in the best of clothes and ornaments they had. A lot of them accompanied the queen mothers, the ministers and the gurus to the outskirts of Ayodhya.

Nandigram, a village on the outskirts of Ayodhya, had become the hub of the administrative affairs as Bharata operated from there. He never stepped into Ayodhya as he had vowed not to enter the city until his brother returned. He never accepted any royal paraphernalia or clothing and not even the facilities offered to a king. He lived in abject poverty and hardly ate or slept. But though he did not accept the rights of the king, he fully executed the responsibilities of the king.191

Crowds began to swell at Nandigram expecting their prince to return anytime now. After making all the arrangements for a grand welcome in Ayodhya, Shatrughna returned to Bharata. Bharata was holding the paduka of Rama, which had been installed on the throne of Ayodhya. Every single day Bharata would report to the footwear of Lord Rama.192 By doing that he felt he was loyal to Rama.193

People lit thousands of torches and held lamps in their hands. All across Nandigram, lamps placed in decorative patterns. It was dark by then, and everyone knew that Rama was coming through the aerial route. They wanted to give him a spectacular welcome with a show of lights. They waited with bated breath as they glanced at the dark moonlit sky for any signs of the plane.

Then they suddenly saw it—the huge bright moon in conjunction with the pushya constellation in the background194 and the majestic pushpak vimana. Rama was descending gracefully from the skies, almost He was the full moon itself. It was dark, but because of the bright sky, everyone could see the beautiful moonlike face of Lord Rama in front of the pushpak vimana. Right next to Him were the most gorgeous, divine Mother Sita and the ever-dutiful Lakshmana. Flanking the three were Sugriva, Hanuman, Vibhishan and Jambavan. When Rama glanced below at Nandigram, He was so touched. The view was spell-binding. The lights that were arranged so artistically had created such an effect that it brought a beautiful smile to Rama’s face. He felt so welcome and wanted. Fourteen years ago, He was rejected and exiled. Those had been painful days for all of them. But this welcome and the love reflected off the lights and lamps were enough to help Him forget the trauma of the past.

Darkness to Light

Darkness represents ignorance, which is the property of misguided intelligence. Light represents knowledge, which is a sign of an enlightened intelligence.

When Rama left, Ayodhya was submerged in the darkness of ignorance sponsored by Manthara’s envy and Keikeyi’s stubbornness. By lighting lamps to greet Lord Rama, the citizens demonstrated that the dark phase of Ayodhya was over and light had dawned upon the city with the arrival of their source of hope. Even to this day, this event is celebrated with people lighting up their homes to welcome Rama and hope into their lives.

The brothers were so excited to see their relatives after such a long period. They could hardly wait for the descent to complete. As soon as the vimana landed, Rama and Lakshmana darted into the loving embrace of Bharata and Shatrughna, and the brothers shed joyful tears. Meanwhile, Sita met the three mothers. Rama and Lakshmana offered their respects to their guru Vasistha and Vamadeva. Before paying obeisance to guru Vasistha, Rama did something very intriguing. He took off His bow and unfastened his quiver of arrows and kept them next to the feet of his guru and then paid obeisance. But when He stood up, He did not pick up the weapons. Initially people thought He had forgotten to pick them up. Shatrughna pointed it out. Rama said “The purpose of the weapons is over. Now the kingdom will be ruled not based on weapons, but based on the wisdom of the scriptures.”195

They offered Their prostrated obeisance to the three mothers. Keikeyi was feeling so guilty about being responsible for a fourteenyear mess. But Rama was extra-sensitive while talking to her and paid more attention toward helping her deal with her guilt. Then Rama went on to meet the ministers of Ayodhya headed by Sumanthara. Finally, he walked up to the thousands of citizens waiting for a glimpse of their beloved Prince. Rama spent sufficient time with each individual. He had a personal connect with almost everyone.196

After spending sufficient time with the residents of Ayodhya, Rama returned to the core group. He introduced Sugriva to Bharata and Shatrughna as their fifth brother. Then went on to introduce Hanuman with great love, Vibhishan with great respect and Jambavan with great gratitude. He asked the ministers to ensure that the entire vanara sena be properly taken care of and provided with the best of comforts while they were here with them as guests.

Bridge of Compassion

Lord Rama stepped off the pushpak vimana. When he saw Vasistha standing in front of him, he dropped his bow and offered prostrated obeisance to his guru. But the monkeys do not know anything about discipline, they were very bewildered by the atmosphere around them because all these days they had behaved with Rama as they liked. When He sat down, they sat on the branches all around Him. When the casually behaved vanaras saw the discipline followed in Ayodhya, they were frightened.

Lord Rama understood their fear and immediately called all of them. He was not hesitant or ashamed that the people of Ayodhya would laugh at Him for bringing a band of monkeys as associates. Lord Rama introduced them to everyone in Ayodhya as His friends. He taught them how to fall at the feet of the guru and pay obeisance. This indicated that Rama acted as a bridge between humans and animals.

When the greatest person among mankind (guru Vasistha) and the lowest among animals (the vanaras) faced each other, Lord Rama introduced them to each other. He told the vanaras, “This is Our guru, because of whom We won the battle of Lanka.” And He told guru Vasistha, “These are My friends. To cross an ocean, We require both a ship and a captain. Your blessings became the captain of the ship and these vanaras became the ship. Because of the efforts of both of you We could cross the ocean.”

Lord Rama divided the credit such a peculiar way. On one hand He gave the credit to His guru and on the other hand to the vanaras. To succeed in life you need both blessings and efforts. Rama did not take any credit for Lanka Vijaya. He felt that it was simply the result of the blessings of the guru and the efforts of the vanaras.

Bharata then placed Rama’s paduka next to His feet. Rama wore the footwear indicating that He was ready to assume the role as King of Ayodhya to much celebratory clamor. Bharata declared, “From this moment forth, I am formally offering the reign of the Kingdom of Ayodhya back to Rama to whom it rightfully belonged. I had been overseeing it for the last fourteen years while the Master was away. Now it is time to transfer the rights back to Rama. In the last fourteen years, Ayodhya has grown in its treasury, storehouses, armies and dimension by tenfold.” Bharata had not only done his duties well, but had done it exceedingly well while not accepting any benefit from it.197

Rama was so proud of His brother Bharata. He was always sure that Bharata was a rare gem and now he had proved it beyond doubt. Embracing His brother with great love, Rama expressed His deepest gratitude for setting such a sparkling example of what brotherhood truly meant. Bharata begged Rama to take over the throne immediately. Rama looked at his guru Vasistha who nodded his head in approval. The shy Rama nodded His head in acknowledgment. Instantly, Bharata summoned the barber team. The four brothers had grown dreadlocks since the beginning of the exile period. Post the haircut, the four brothers took a dip in the river, their newly shaved faces shining effulgently like four full moons. The brothers put on their best clothes and jewels. The citizens of Ayodhya were so happy seeing their four princes dressed as royalty. It had given all of them such pain to see their handsome princes dressed as ascetics for all these years. Bharata directed Rama toward a beautiful golden chariot brought in for that grand entry into Ayodhya. After helping Rama ascend the chariot, Bharata took on the reigns himself. Sita also wore Her best clothes with the help of Her three mothers. Rama helped Her up the chariot. Then Shatrughna climbed behind, holding the royal white umbrella over Rama. Finally, Lakshmana and Vibhishan climbed and stood on either side of Sita Rama holding a yak fan, and a peacock feather fan respectively. Numerous elephants followed the royal chariot each hosting the vanara leaders. Thousands of citizens and vanaras followed the procession all the way into the city of Ayodhya.

Although late in the night, the number of lights and torches lit in the city made it feel like day. The citizens of Ayodhya had lined up the streets to welcome their beloved prince back. At every little juncture, the chariot stopped as citizens wanted to perform a little dance, or a little song for the pleasure of their prince. So many elderly ladies would come ahead and perform the ceremonial aarti invoking auspiciousness for the royal family. Children held out flowers for Rama and Sita. The magnanimous couple accepted all their love gracefully. Musical instruments were played all through their entry into the city. Brahmanas chanted Vedic hymns. People waved flags from their terraces—so many were waving colorful clothes and flags.198

Rama instructed Bharata to offer Sugriva His own palace. Bharata caught Sugriva by his hand and proceeded toward Rama’s palace with Sugriva’s personal assistant and bodyguards. The guests were offered the best of comforts and luxuries. The citizens of Ayodhya joined in with the royal family to take care of the hundreds and thousands of guests that had come along with Rama. The cooperative spirit was palpable in Ayodhya. The city remained awake through the night.199

Two pairs of eyes were witnessing the celebration of Ayodhya from the terrace. A celebration of this magnitude hadn’t happened in the last fourteen years. Exactly fourteen years before a celebration exactly of the scale that these eyes were witnessing now was seen before too. At that time these pair of eyes burned with envy. But now there was a change in demeanor. What she perceived to be good for her queen turned out to be a disaster. She felt responsible for all that pain now. Finally, her lips smiled seeing the reentrance of happiness into Ayodhya tonight. Manthara ran down to join in the celebrations.

Festival of Lights

Diwali is the festival that commemorates the entry of Rama into Ayodhya. It represents six things which can be remembered through the acronym DIWALI.

DETERMINATION: Rama’s fourteen-year struggle reveals His determination to install the right value systems in the hearts of Ayodhya’s citizens. Although wronged, Rama still followed the orders of His father, who was the king of Ayodhya at the time. The only reason He followed the instructions even though it was completely absurd was His desire to show the citizens of Ayodhya what it meant to be an ideal follower before becoming an ideal leader. Even though so many tried to undo His decision, Rama remained steadfast about showing the right conduct of an ideal follower. Rama Rajya was built on a foundation of won’t power (resisting the wrong) in addition to just will power (embracing the right).

ILLUMINATE: The lights in every nook and corner of Ayodhya indicated a promise by the citizens that from hereon decisions in the city would not be taken during emotional darkness but in the light of knowledge and clarity. The decision to banish Rama and crown Bharata was taken not just in the darkness of the night but also in the dark environment created by Keikeyi under the influence of Manthara. While the whole of Ayodhya slept, that dark plot was hatched. Fourteen years later when Rama was landing back in Ayodhya in the pushpak vimana, the whole city was lit up to indicate that the light of knowledge would now prevail and no decisions would be taken in the emotional darkness of life.

WINNING TIP: By his actions, Bharata showed that winning hearts was more important than winning thrones. The game of life was more important than the game of thrones and that relationships had to be prioritized over resource-ships. He was given the throne of Ayodhya on a golden platter. With the death of his father and absence of knowledge of the whereabouts of his brother, Bharata could have easily justified that he had no option but to reluctantly accept the throne that needed a king that could protect it. Instead, Bharata chose the tough path of trying to find Rama and even tougher path of trying to convince Him to return to Ayodhya to take charge. Bharata prioritized his relationship with his brother over an opulent kingdom.

ACCOMMODATE: When Rama was returning to Ayodhya, he borrowed the pushpak vimana from Vibhishan because He had to traverse a huge distance back to Ayodhya in one night. Rama invited all the male monkeys to join Him in the plane back to Ayodhya. Sensing Rama’s desire, the pushpak vimana expanded to accommodate so many. When they reached near Kishkinda, Sita expressed Her desire that it was only fair that the female monkeys also be allowed to witness the coronation ceremony in Ayodhya along with the male monkeys. Rama agreed and the female monkeys boarded the plane, which expanded even further to accommodate the new entrants. So far, Ravana had used it only for solo pleasure rides and now Rama was accommodating so many. Every time more people entered it, the plane expanded to create more space. The ability to accommodate all kinds of people in our lives comes from the desire to accommodate.

LET GO: Rama did not tolerate any insult to Keikeyi and made sure that no one referred to the past even subtly and sarcastically. He decided to let go off the past. In fact, He gave her more attention and love than ever before. Letting go off others’ lapses is the best way to constantly remind them of it.

INDEBTEDNESS: During the coronation ceremony, Rama noticed Sita crying. Sensing some disturbance in Her mind, He asked to share Her sorrow. Sita mentioned that a few minutes ago as a part of the celebrations, someone threw some red powder in the air and that it reminded Her of Jatayu’s blood splash from when Ravana slashed his wings. Sita was weeping in remembrance of Jatayu’s sacrifice. She reminded Rama that behind their success today were the sacrifices of so many heroes And that it was important to be grateful while celebrating, knowing that the success in hand was due to the sacrifices of many.

Diwali essentially means being determined to illuminate our lives by understanding that you win by accommodating others and by letting go of their lapses while being indebted for the contributions of many.

coronation day

Five hundred vanaras set out of Ayodhya that morning with golden pots in their hands. They were assigned the task of visiting five hundred different rivers across the world and bringing back a potful of water in time for the coronation ceremony. Jambavan, Hanuman, Gavaya and Rishabha were sent to bring a pot each of water from the four oceans. Soon five hundred and four sacred pots were in front of guru Vasistha. The elaborate coronation ceremony began with guru Vasistha leading a team of brahmanas in the chanting of mantras and purification of ingredients to be used in the sacred ceremony. Rama and Sita received a grand welcome. They ascended the throne and sat on it together as the King and Queen of Ayodhya. Sage Vasistha placed the royal crown on the head of Lord Rama, declaring him to be the king of Ayodhya from that moment on. The celestials from the heavens had also been invited to be part of the coronation ceremony. The host of the most prominent of the gods was also there witnessing the most auspicious event with great joy. Each offered the most invaluable gifts to the new King and Queen of the Koshala Kingdom and the celebrated Ikshvaku dynasty.

On the completion of the formal ceremony, the heavenly gandharvas and apsaras gave their best performance to please Lord Rama and Mother Sita. During a celebratory dance, Rama noticed Sita crying. She was reminded of Jatayu and his sacrifice. She told Rama to remember that Their success was built on the backs of the sacrifices of many heroes. Rama was moved by this. How sensitive and grateful Sita is! Rama thought. His love for Her just increased manifold.200

A thanksgiving ceremony was organized right after the coronation ceremony. So many people had served Sita and Rama with selflessness, but They hadn’t been able to reciprocate the gratitude adequately as They had nothing in Their possession to give away. Of course, They shared the love They had within Their hearts, but now that They had the resources, They wanted to offer more. Every friend was called and duly honored. Rama announced that though each one of them was being awarded, the value of the gift was insignificant compared to the valuable service they had rendered in the past months. They chose to offer the last recognition to the most submissive and heroic personality who had, by now, become Their favorite. When he was called, Hanuman walked up to the throne with his head bent low cast as Rama and Sita recollected tales of his heroism. Everyone in Ayodhya was spellbound by his achievements.201

Rama embraced Hanuman and told the world that He could never sufficiently repay His debt to Hanuman. Sita declared that Hanuman deserved more honor than anyone else, and that She definitely wanted to offer a reward to the reluctant Hanuman. Hanuman reiterated that for him, an opportunity to be of service to Lord Rama was his greatest reward. Seeing Hanuman’s hesitance in accepting anything, Sita decided to offer him Her own priceless ornate necklace. When Sita actually took off Her invaluable necklace, people in the audience gasped. They praised Hanuman for receiving such an honor from the Queen and considered him lucky.

Hanuman held out his hands and received the gift with absolute disinterest. He began to closely inspect the necklace from all angles. After twisting and turning it in all directions, Hanuman still couldn’t see what he wanted to see. To the shock of all those present there, he then began to dismantle the necklace, tearing it to small shreds. He broke each and every diamond in the necklace and carefully reviewed its inner content. He began to smash the pieces of diamonds on the ground in disgust. Finally, after every part of the necklace was inspected and demolished, Hanuman looked up. The gaping people in the audience could not digest what had just happened. How could anyone destroy a royal gift? That too, right in front of the Queen! When they saw that Hanuman wasn’t even attempting to defend himself by justifying his actions, they became extremely upset and angry with him for his deplorable behavior. All the prominent citizens of Ayodhya from different corners of the courtroom began to demand an explanation.

Hanuman explained his seemingly irrational act. “I was breaking the stones to see if Mother Sita and Lord Rama were inside the necklace.” For Hanuman, something was valuable only if it had the presence of the divine couple. And since the necklace and every diamond in that necklace were devoid of the couple’s presence, the gift was useless for him. When Lord Rama and Sita heard this explanation, they were extremely pleased with Hanuman’s devotion and smiled at each other. For Them, he was Their real diamond. But the others in the courtroom felt that their King and Queen had been insulted by Hanuman’s action. Some even began to joke about Hanuman’s so-called devotion. They even said that his love for Lord Rama and Mother Sita was only a sham and had no real depth.202

One of them mocked Hanuman. He said, “If you really don’t value anything that doesn’t have Lord Rama and Mother Sita in it, will you value your heart if it doesn’t have Lord Rama and Mother Sita in it? How can you be sure that your heart has the divine couple inside it?” Everyone burst out laughing at Hanuman’s shrunken face. The laughter suddenly stopped as everyone froze… Hanuman was standing in the middle of the courtroom with his chest ripped open, blood spilling over the courtroom floor. People were aghast at what he had done to himself. With his bare hands, he had ripped apart his chest and within his chest cavity was a beautiful image of Sita and Lord Rama. Lord Rama and Sita had tears in Their eyes. The mockers felt ashamed and had been silenced. Everyone in the courtroom fell at the feet of their superhero and bhakta Hanuman.203

righteous rule

Rama met the vanara army for the last time. The royal family had accompanied these great vanaras till the outskirts of Ayodhya to bid them adieu. The parting experience was a very painful one. Every vanara came forward, paid his obeisance to Lord Rama and accepted a special gift from His hands and stepped back. Lord Rama had decided to greet every monkey in the army before the sendoff. The previous day, the chiefs of the army had been felicitated. But Lord Rama felt that wasn’t enough. He wanted to express His gratitude even to the lowest-ranking soldiers in the army as all of them had done more than what any family member would do. With the felicitation ceremony done, He turned to the entire senior batch of monkeys and instructed them to return to their abodes and continue serving society, according to the principles they had learned under His guidance in the last few months. All the monkeys were in tears as they departed from Ayodhya. None wanted to return to Kishkinda, but they did so because of Rama’s instruction to return and run the kingdom on the principles of Rama Rajya.

Rama next turned to Vibhishan. King Vibhishan did not want to leave Ayodhya and return to Lanka. Understanding his attachment, Rama gave him a gift that immediately made him agree to return. He offered him the most precious treasure of Ayodhya, the family deity of the entire Ikshvaku dynasty, Lord Ranganath whose facial features resembled Lord Rama’s almost to perfection. By carrying Ranganath back with him, Vibhishan felt he was literally carrying Lord Rama back with him.

Family Heirloom

As Vibhishan carried the deity of Ranganath by air to Lanka, he passed over a tract of land flanked by a river. This was Sri Rangam where the Kaveri river flowed on either side, making this land extremely beautiful and holy. When Ranganath saw the beauty of this most auspicious place, he felt inclined to stay there. By his divine will, Vibhishan felt an urgent need to answer nature’s call. He descended immediately and paced the deity on the ground on a huge rock and ran off. When he returned more peaceful, he tried to lift the deity back again to continue his journey. But he was just unable to. The deity had become so heavy that it was just impossible for him to even budge it. He understood that the Lord wanted to stay here. He respected the choice and agreed to let go. Ranganath was pleased with Vibhishan’s mood and did something unimaginable. Usually deities face the east. This is probably the only deity that faces the south, the direction of Vibhishan’s kingdom, to show his gratitude to Vibhishan for all his services.

Lord Rama, Lakshmana, Sita and Hanuman were the only ones left. Lord Rama turned to Hanuman, and with tears in his eyes, asked him if he had any desire in his heart. Hanuman, for the first time, decided to express his desire to the Lord. He said, “Please grant me the boon that my love for You remains forever. Let me not forget You even for a second. Let my attention be not diverted from Your exclusive shelter and service. Let me not even aspire to serve anyone else in this world who is disconnected to You. Let me continue living till such time that Your divine story is told and retold in this world. I do not seek anything else.” Hearing Hanuman’s words, filled with a sincere desire to serve him, Lord Rama became overwhelmed with emotions and while embracing him, shared his heart. Rama reiterated He could only offer Hanuman for everything he had done and for everything he would be doing in the future was this embrace.

Rama had asked everyone else to return home, but He did not say that to Hanuman. He wanted Hanuman to stay on with Him in Ayodhya. Of course, Hanuman was most thrilled with the way things had turned out for him. Sita, Rama, Lakshmana and Hanuman returned to the city that was beaming with joy.

Rama ruled Ayodhya for the next eleven thousand years. The reign of Rama was famously known as Rama Rajya. It was a time when no one was sad—a time of great joy and prosperity. Anyone with any problem was allowed to freely approach Rama, and He ensured that their concerns were addressed. Great sacrifices were performed to perfection for the welfare of the world. The kingdom saw no diseases and no thieves. No one became a widow during that golden period. No one felt worthless. Parents never saw the death of their children. People lived very long lives with perfect health and prosperity. Trees and plants offered the tastiest fruits and no insects or pests ever damaged crops. Rains were good as was the weather. Not just humans, but even wild animals gave up their cruelty for peace. Everyone in every direction spoke about Rama alone. The whole world had become Rama mayi, inundated with Rama.

Gayatri Mantra

Rama Rajya is built on the spirit of sacrifice and selfless service to society. Leadership means responsibility, responsibility means sacrifice, sacrifice means tears. In Rama Rajya, a leader is willing to make any sacrifice for the happiness and well-being of his followers.

There are twenty-four letters in the Gayatri mantra and there are twenty-four thousand shlokas in the Valmiki Ramayana. The first letter of every thousandth shloka of the Ramayana together forms the Gayatri mantra, making this revered mantra the essence of the epic. While the Gayatri mantra was first mentioned in the Rig Veda, it has gained significance thanks to the Ramayana.

Virtues of Rama Katha

Lava and Kusha had held their audience captive throughout the narration. No one even moved or breathed. Even Rama was spellbound by His own story. They closed their narration by eulogizing the benefits of hearing Rama katha.

They offered seven benedictions to those who immerse themselves in the story of Ramayana.

Anyone who listens to this foremost of epics written by the great sage Valmiki will be endowed with righteousness, fame, longevity and success.

An attentive hearer will be relieved of all misfortune, and all obstacles will come to an end.

Those bereft of children, but desirous will be bestowed with offspring.

Those seeking wealth will receive wealth in abundance.

Lord Rama will be extremely pleased with those who attentively listen to the narration or read the Ramayana.

The one who writes the Ramayana will achieve the highest destination possible.

This epic will always be treasured by those who seek wisdom, intelligence, good association, welfare, brilliance and fame.

## About the Author

Shubha Vilas is a TEDx speaker, lifestyle coach, storyteller and author. He studied patent law after completing his engineering degree but, finally, chose the path of a spiritual seeker.

Ramayana: The Game of Life is his bestselling series. He is also the author of Open-Eyed Meditations, Mystical Tales for a Magical Life, Perfect Love, Hanuman Chalisa, amongst others. The focus of his work is the application of scriptural wisdom in everyday living, addressing the needs of corporates and youth through thought-provoking seminars. He has delivered more than 4000 lectures worldwide and is a visiting faculty at the Indian Institute of Management.

To know more about him, visit www.shubhavilas.com
